Do You Really Need a GPU to Guard Your LLM? CPU-Class Classifiers and Multi-Stage Pipelines for Safety Enforcement at Scale
Authors: Vasudev Majhi, Dhruv Gupta, Advait Singh, Matthew Barker, Dhruv Kumar
First: 2025-12-22T04:00:35+00:00 · Latest: 2026-06-13T05:46:54+00:00
Comments: Under Review. 25 pages, 5 figures, 38 tables
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Safety classifiers that screen LLM inputs for jailbreak attempts have become standard deployment components, yet almost all production systems rely on GPU-based models: fine-tuned transformers and LLM-as-a-judge pipelines. These approaches impose significant per-query latency and infrastructure cost. Very little research has asked whether CPU-based classifiers, such as support vector machines and gradient-boosted trees trained on TF-IDF features, can match their accuracy across the conditions that production deployments encounter. We evaluate five CPU classifier families, Mamba-130M as an SSM-based GPU classifier, and transformer-based GPU models (DeBERTa-v3 and Gemma-2B with LoRA) across nine jailbreak sources and three regimes: in-distribution (D1), out-of-distribution (D2), and adversarially obfuscated (D3). On D1, the best CPU classifier matches the best transformer GPU model at roughly one-fifth the deployment cost. On D2, CPU classifiers fail via confident miscalibration, producing high-confidence false negatives that bypass escalation entirely. On D3, CPU classifiers outperform transformer GPU models by more than 26 percentage points in F1. Based on these complementary failure modes, we design GuardChain, a three-stage safety pipeline (Regex -> CPU -> GPU) that routes each prompt to the cheapest stage capable of a confident decision. The CPU stage alone resolves 80\% of in-distribution prompts at near-peak accuracy, and the GPU stage recovers the out-of-distribution failures. For practitioners deploying LLM safety at scale, this work provides evidence that GPU-class infrastructure is unnecessary for the majority of traffic.
中文标题/摘要
标题：您真的需要 GPU 来保护您的 LLM 吗？用于安全执行的 CPU 类分类器和多阶段管道
用于筛查 LLM 输入以防止越狱尝试的安全分类器已成为标准部署组件，但几乎所有生产系统都依赖于基于 GPU 的模型：微调的变换器和 LLM 作为裁判的管道。这些方法在每个查询上都带来了显著的延迟和基础设施成本。很少有研究探讨基于 CPU 的分类器，如支持向量机和基于 TF-IDF 特征训练的梯度提升树，是否能在生产部署所遇到的条件下匹配其准确性。 我们评估了五种 CPU 分类器系列，Mamba-130M 作为基于 SSM 的 GPU 分类器，以及基于变换器的 GPU 模型（DeBERTa-v3 和 Gemma-2B 与 LoRA）在九个越狱来源和三个模式下的表现：分布内（D1）、分布外（D2）和对抗性模糊（D3）。在 D1 中，最佳 CPU 分类器的表现与最佳变换器 GPU 模型相当，部署成本约为其五分之一。在 D2 中，CPU 分类器因自信的错误校准而失败，产生高置信度的假阴性，完全绕过升级。在 D3 中，CPU 分类器在 F1 上超越变换器 GPU 模型超过 26 个百分点。 基于这些互补的失败模式，我们设计了 GuardChain，一个三阶段安全管道（Regex -> CPU -> GPU），将每个提示路由到能够做出自信决策的最便宜阶段。仅 CPU 阶段就以接近峰值的准确率解决了 80% 的分布内提示，而 GPU 阶段则恢复了分布外的失败。对于大规模部署 LLM 安全的从业者而言，这项工作提供了证据，表明 GPU 类基础设施对于大多数流量来说并非必要。
Summary / 总结

Previous methods primarily utilized fine-tuned transformers and LLM-as-a-judge pipelines, which often resulted in high operational costs and latency, prompting the exploration of CPU-based classifiers like support vector machines and gradient-boosted trees. The proposed approach, GuardChain, introduces a three-stage safety pipeline that effectively combines CPU and GPU classifiers, demonstrating that CPU classifiers can match the accuracy of GPU models at a fraction of the cost for in-distribution prompts, while also outperforming them in adversarial scenarios. The methodology involves evaluating various CPU classifier families against GPU models across multiple jailbreak conditions, achieving a notable performance where the CPU stage resolves 80% of in-distribution prompts with high accuracy, thus supporting the goal of reducing infrastructure costs in LLM safety enforcement.

NeST: Neuron Selective Tuning for LLM Safety
Authors: Sasha Behrouzi, Lichao Wu, Mohamadreza Rostami, Ahmad-Reza Sadeghi
First: 2026-02-18T20:01:01+00:00 · Latest: 2026-06-12T11:06:43+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Safety alignment is essential for the responsible deployment of Large Language Models (LLMs). Yet, existing approaches often rely on heavyweight fine-tuning that is costly to update, audit, and maintain across model families. Full fine-tuning incurs substantial computational and storage overhead, while parameter-efficient methods, e.g., Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA), trade efficiency for inconsistent safety gains and sensitivity to design choices. Safety intervention mechanisms reduce unsafe outputs without modifying model weights, but do not directly shape or preserve the internal representations that govern safety behavior. We present NeST, a Neuron-Selective Tuning framework for efficient post-hoc safety alignment. NeST identifies safety-relevant feed-forward neurons via activation probing on vanilla harmful and benign prompts, clusters neurons with similar activation profiles, and trains shared cluster-level updates while freezing the rest of the model. Importantly, NeST is trained only on vanilla malicious prompts, without using jailbreak-specific attack data, yet generalizes robustly to diverse jailbreaks. The learned updates are then folded into the original weights, incurring no inference-time overhead. Evaluated on 14 open-weight language and multimodal models, NeST outperforms lightweight baselines and approaches full fine-tuning robustness with significantly fewer trainable parameters. On text-only models, NeST reduces average jailbreak attack success rate from 44.5% to 1.1% while training only 0.4M parameters on average. Across multimodal settings, it reduces ASR from 55.3% to 1.1%, and for downstream fine-tuned variants, it restores safety by reducing ASR from 53.8% to 0.8%. These results show that robust, maintainable safety alignment can be achieved by concentrating adaptation on localized, functionally coherent safety structures.
中文标题/摘要
标题：NeST：用于大型语言模型安全的神经元选择性调优
安全对齐对于大型语言模型（LLMs）的负责任部署至关重要。然而，现有方法通常依赖于成本高昂的重调优，难以在模型家族中进行更新、审计和维护。完全调优会产生大量的计算和存储开销，而参数高效的方法，如低秩适应（LoRA），则在效率与安全收益不一致和对设计选择敏感之间进行权衡。安全干预机制在不修改模型权重的情况下减少不安全输出，但并未直接塑造或保留影响安全行为的内部表示。 我们提出了NeST，一种用于高效事后安全对齐的神经元选择性调优框架。NeST通过对普通有害和良性提示的激活探测，识别与安全相关的前馈神经元，聚类具有相似激活特征的神经元，并在冻结模型其余部分的同时训练共享的聚类级更新。重要的是，NeST仅在普通恶意提示上进行训练，而不使用特定于越狱的攻击数据，但能够稳健地推广到多种越狱场景。学习到的更新随后被折叠到原始权重中，不会增加推理时的开销。在14个开放权重的语言和多模态模型上进行评估，NeST的表现优于轻量级基线，并以显著更少的可训练参数接近完全调优的鲁棒性。在仅文本模型上，NeST将平均越狱攻击成功率从44.5%降低到1.1%，同时平均仅训练0.4M参数。在多模态设置中，它将ASR从55.3%降低到1.1%，对于下游微调变体，它通过将ASR从53.8%降低到0.8%来恢复安全。这些结果表明，通过集中适应于局部、功能一致的安全结构，可以实现稳健、可维护的安全对齐。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the critical need for safety alignment in the deployment of Large Language Models (LLMs), highlighting the limitations of existing methods such as full fine-tuning, which is resource-intensive, and parameter-efficient approaches like Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA), which compromise safety consistency. The proposed NeST (Neuron-Selective Tuning) framework offers a novel solution by identifying safety-relevant neurons through activation probing and clustering them for targeted updates, thereby avoiding the overhead associated with full model fine-tuning. This method is motivated by the need for efficient safety alignment without modifying model weights and has been shown to generalize effectively to various attack scenarios. The methodology involves training on harmful prompts while freezing the rest of the model, resulting in significant performance improvements; NeST reduces the average jailbreak attack success rate from 44.5% to 1.1% with only 0.4M trainable parameters, and similarly achieves robust safety in multimodal settings, demonstrating that effective safety alignment can be achieved with minimal computational cost.
本研究关注大型语言模型（LLM）部署中安全对齐的关键需求，强调现有方法的局限性，这些方法要么需要大量的微调，要么通过参数高效技术妥协安全收益。提出的NeST框架引入了一种神经元选择性调优的方法，通过识别和聚类与安全相关的神经元，同时冻结模型的其余部分，从而有效地对齐安全，避免了全微调带来的计算开销。该方法的动机在于需要一种更可维护和有效的安全干预，而不修改模型权重。NeST通过实现最小参数更新的稳健安全对齐做出了重要贡献，在各种模型中显示出监狱攻击成功率的显著降低，包括文本模型从44.5%降至1.1%，多模态设置从55.3%降至1.1%，从而支持其高效安全增强的目标。
HarmProfile: Characterizing Harmful Distributions in Frontier LLMs
Authors: Zhouyuan Ma, Yutao Wu, Hanxun Huang, Xiang Zheng, Xiao Liu, Yixin Cao, Zuxuan Wu, Xingjun Ma, Yu-Gang Jiang
First: 2026-06-11T09:39:32+00:00 · Latest: 2026-06-11T09:39:32+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
Frontier large language models (LLMs) safety evaluation has largely treated harmful generation as an attack outcome rather than as an object of analysis. Consequently, little is known about the harmful outputs produced during model misbehavior, partly because large-scale, high-quality collections of frontier-LLM misbehavior are difficult to obtain. To address this gap, we introduce HarmProfile, a content-centric benchmark dataset that collects model misbehavior across diverse harm categories and model families, and defines the resulting harmful-output distribution as a model-level risk profile. The premise is that, just as linguistic behavior can be characterized from an utterance corpus, model risk can be characterized from the content, severity, and variation of its safety failures. HarmProfile contains over 80,000 validated artifacts from 23 frontier LLMs across 13 model families, organized into 15 harm categories and 57 subcategories. Using this corpus, we find that frontier LLMs reliably produce harmful content at scale, yet exhibit distinct risk profiles; both harmfulness and diversity grow with model capability, suggesting that frontier LLMs may appear safe yet harbor increasingly dangerous knowledge beneath the alignment surface. Our source code is available at https://github.com/fresh-ma/HarmProfile .
中文标题/摘要
标题：HarmProfile：前沿大型语言模型中有害分布的特征化
前沿大型语言模型（LLMs）的安全评估在很大程度上将有害生成视为攻击结果，而非分析对象。因此，对于模型失常期间产生的有害输出知之甚少，部分原因是难以获得大规模、高质量的前沿LLM失常数据集。为了解决这一问题，我们引入了HarmProfile，这是一个以内容为中心的基准数据集，收集了来自不同有害类别和模型家族的模型失常，并将结果有害输出分布定义为模型级风险概况。前提是，正如语言行为可以从话语语料库中表征，模型风险可以从其安全失效的内容、严重性和变化中表征。HarmProfile包含来自13个模型家族的23个前沿LLM的超过80,000个经过验证的文档，组织成15个有害类别和57个子类别。利用该语料库，我们发现前沿LLM在规模上可靠地产生有害内容，但表现出不同的风险特征；有害性和多样性随着模型能力的提高而增长，这表明前沿LLM可能看似安全，但在对齐表面下潜藏着越来越危险的知识。我们的源代码可在https://github.com/fresh-ma/HarmProfile获取。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the lack of comprehensive analysis regarding harmful outputs generated by frontier large language models (LLMs), which have primarily been viewed as attack outcomes rather than subjects of study. Previous methods have struggled with the collection of large-scale, high-quality data on model misbehavior, leading to insufficient understanding of harmful distributions. The proposed approach, HarmProfile, offers a content-centric benchmark dataset that categorizes model misbehavior across various harm categories and model families, effectively characterizing harmful-output distributions as model-level risk profiles. This method is well-motivated as it allows for a systematic analysis of the severity and variation of safety failures in LLMs. The contribution of the paper lies in the creation of a dataset containing over 80,000 validated artifacts from 23 frontier LLMs, revealing that while these models can produce harmful content reliably, they also exhibit distinct risk profiles, with harmfulness and diversity increasing alongside model capability, indicating potential hidden risks beneath their alignment. The findings support the goal of better understanding and mitigating risks associated with LLMs.
本研究解决了前沿大型语言模型（LLMs）生成的有害输出缺乏理解的问题，这些模型主要被评估为攻击结果，而非分析对象。以往的方法在收集高质量的模型失误数据方面存在困难，导致对有害生成的见解不足。提出的方法HarmProfile引入了一个以内容为中心的基准数据集，分类了不同危害类别和模型家族的模型失误，从而允许对有害输出分布进行全面表征，作为模型级别的风险概况。该方法收集了来自23个前沿LLMs的超过80,000个经过验证的文献，揭示了这些模型能够大规模生成有害内容，同时也表现出随着模型能力增加而增强的独特风险特征。研究结果表明，前沿LLMs可能看似安全，但在其对齐表面下潜藏着越来越危险的知识，从而支持了对安全评估进行更细致分析的必要性。
